# Claims ledger

**Legal-source audit (2026-10-22).** [SOURCE] The [interoperability register](interoperability.md)
now identifies primary legal texts, access dates and provision/paragraph references (L1–L4, P1–P3,
S1), replacing the earlier memory-based account. Its application to R-001 is labelled separately
from verified source content; national-law and copied-content questions remain [UNKNOWN]. This
audit is not new technical or in-game evidence for the spline candidate.

Game paths are relative to the installation root listed in [inventory](game-install-inventory.md). `[V-GAME]` reports observed startup/log behavior only, unless a row says otherwise. Dates are per row.

| C38 | Height and material decal layers are LZAV format-3 streams (prefix 0x35), one per 132×132 tile (128 + 2-pixel borders copied from neighbours, edge pixels repeated at the image boundary), stored south-up in row-major order: 64×32 tiles at level 0, then mip levels down to one tile (2,731 tiles). Mip level k+1 takes the odd pixels of level k. Re-tiling decoded data reproduces all 2,048 level-0 tiles byte-for-byte; some vanilla lower mips differ (they appear stale). | C54 | Material lists are shorter than 16 for most biomes: of 191 lists in `gfx/terrain2/materials.txt`, 93 have 13 entries, 30 have 14, 8 have 12, 5 have 15, and `arid_plateau_desert_biome` has 1; only 33 have 16. The engine shader `clausewitz/.../cw/terrain2_materials.fxh` clamps the layer value to 15 (`min(MaterialMask, 15)`, so 16 and 255 render as 15) and reads `_Biomes[biome]._Materials[slot]` from a fixed 64-entry table; material 0 is `fallback_material` (dead-grass diffuse). Unlisted entries therefore most likely render `fallback_material` [INFERRED]. Vanilla still writes slots 13–15 into 13-entry biomes (about 40% of their pixels, e.g. 42% in `arid_hills_desert_biome`, all of whose 13 entries are `base_sand`). `biomes.txt` has no rule for desert wetlands, so those templates resolve to `default_biome` (one entry, `fallback_material`); vanilla has 10 such locations | [V-LOCAL]/[V-EXEC] | Shader include; `materials.txt`; per-biome slot histograms over all 16 decals (scratch probe) | 1.4.1 / build 25905365 | 2026-10-19 |

(1) Rivers are not spread alike: with river size taken as the rank of the widest `rivers.png` pixel (codes 4, 5, 11, 15 → 1–4, markers alone → 1) [INFERRED], vanilla American cities, towns, state capitals and members have a river in 111, 375, 463 and 513‰ of cases (mean size 0.11, 0.50, 0.78, 0.64), generated ones (three worlds) in 857–1000, 750–1000, 702–795 and 666–777‰ (1.29–2.00, 1.00–2.00, 1.19–1.53, 1.18–1.48), because hearths and population follow river farming; leaving `river = 0.5` × size out of the estimate biased the calibration. (2) The physical part over all land matches vanilla (−4.26; generated −4.46 and −4.00 on continents 515055336 and archipelago 5), but generated civilisations hold the best land (state capitals −1.99 and −1.32 against −6.24) and generated cities and towns stand on poorer land than the rest of generated state land, while vanilla cities stand on better land (+0.94 against capitals −6.24); one state-land curve cannot reach both class means (C75: cities 7.8–9.7, towns 3.1–6.6). A term from the hearth influence before poor land damps it made cities worse (7.2–8.3) and was dropped. The saturation is now fitted per class: cities, towns, other state land. (3) Tribes and unorganised land stay at 0.12–0.38 against 1.26 and 0.78: generated tribal land is poorer than vanilla's (−4.30 to −4.64 against −3.25) and vanilla gives non-state land authored regional bonuses; a tribal fit would need the full term on all tribal land with complexity above 0.25 and still reach only 0.56–1.11, so none is made. (4) The criterion "25–45% of land with a positive term" measured the hearth count: on continents 3717486232 (3 hearths) only 229‰ of land has any complexity; it is replaced by coverage, the share of land reached by a hearth that has a term (≥ 850‰). | C127 | **Corrected by C135: what the shaders read at country selection is every location unknown, not known; the reading of the fill below may stand, but the texture is not filled there.** How the engine decides what TI covers (static reading of the disassembly of `eu5.exe`, RVAs; names are mine). `CTerraIncognitaManager::Update` (0x6ba6050) runs once per view per frame (called from 0x7162ee0), behind a global switch (the `Draw.TerraIncognita` toggle, [INFERRED]) and an interface state test. It compares three location maps of the manager (offsets 0x60, 0xd0, 0x140) with the ones last uploaded and, when they differ, calls `CTerraIncognitaGraphics::UpdateBuffer` (0x6ba5920), which writes one byte per location into the visibility texture: 0xFF for the first map (visible), 0x01 and 0x02 for the others (the shader's mode bits). The maps are filled at 0x6ba5510 from the **viewing country**, resolved by 0x23d7310: a forced "observed country" of the interface if its flag is set, else the **local player's country**. With a valid country its known locations go into the first map; **with none or an invalid one the first map is set to all ones, that is, everything is known**. That is why the New World is open at country selection (no player country yet) and covered in a campaign (the player's country knows only its `discovered_areas`). The manager also re-reads the define `NTerraIncognita.SHADER_PATH` at run time and rebuilds its graphics object when the string changes. So TI at selection is the engine's own TI lacking a viewer, not a different tool. Open: whether the lobby's clicked country becomes the viewing country (`GetPlayerOrLobbyCountry` suggests the lobby country is kept apart from the player's, so probably not), and what sets the forced observed country (observers; `change_player`, `set_observed_playable` and the observer commands exist) | [V-LOCAL]/[INFERRED] | `local_data/re/` helpers (PE reader, RTTI, `.pdata`, `objdump`; not committed); RVAs above | 1.4.2 / build 26043558 | 2026-10-21 |

| C162 | The spline network of the map, `in_game/gfx/map/spline_network/spline_network.splnet` (31,615,438 bytes, 1.4.3), is the engine's tagged binary: `0xee` = 4, `0x45a` = { 610,108 points, 110,111 segments, 51,095 strips }, then three lists sorted by id. A point is `{ 0x0b = u32 id, 0x4c = { f32 x, f32 y } }`: an anchor (21,046) has the index of its location in the order of `definitions.txt` counted from 1 (all on their own location of the raster, a median 3.9 pixels from its centroid; x from the left, y from the bottom), the others (589,062) the id `0x10000000 + index` numbered one after another from the highest anchor plus one (27,924). A segment is `{ 0x0b = i64 n << 8, 0x5f7 = { u32 point ids } }`, a strip `{ 0x0b = i64 (higher << 35 \| lower << 6), 0x5f5 = { i64 segment ids } }` whose segments join the lower location's anchor to the higher one's, some walked backwards and 50,879 segments used by one strip, the others shared. The strips cover all 50,580 land-land contacts of vanilla (515 more reach wasteland) and all 1,646 road pairs of `09_roads.txt`; none crosses the map's seam; points lie 2.4 pixels apart at the median, 18 per strip. Vanilla's America is the contiguous index block 19,436–25,602; the mod's definitions keep indexes up to 19,435, move the 2,538 kept locations after the block and put the generated locations among them (indexes 19,436–30,210 in one world). `rnw.splnet` reads the file in 0.8 s and writes it back byte for byte (local test).
